- Frontend Storybook + Vite 8/Rolldown is structurally Vite-coupled (Wave 121 SW7): the codebase deeply uses `import.meta.glob` for i18n + map locale data. Switching `@storybook/react-vite` → `@storybook/react-webpack5` bypasses the W120 `__STORYBOOK_MODULE_*` issue but hits `__webpack_import_meta__.glob is not a function` at runtime. Webpack swap requires either upstream Storybook fix for Vite/Rolldown integration OR source rewrite of i18n + map data loaders to use require.context. SW7 attempted Webpack swap, reverted at T+10 min. Token saved client-side: `chpt_48d051b3688a3e4` (Chromatic project still configured).
- Audit files location convention (Wave 122 polish-docs-v3, `8eba94352`): write new `AUDIT_WAVE<N>.md` in **`docs/audits/`** (active dir, holds last 3 waves), NOT at repo root. When wave **N+3** opens, promote oldest active audit to `docs/audits/archive/` (Variant B reorg). Index of all audits in `docs/audits/INDEX.md` (reverse-chronological). 27 files relocated from root in W122 polish-docs-v3 — fresh `git clone` no longer drowns contributors in audit clutter. `git log --follow` preserves pre-move history. Plain text mentions of `AUDIT_WAVE<N>.md` in CLAUDE.md ## Audit Trail rows + archived audits' internal cross-refs left as descriptive references (not clickable links). Detect-secrets baseline is path-keyed — moving an audit file with example secrets re-flags them; add inline `# pragma: allowlist secret` comments to the moved file (W122 polish-docs-v3 hit this on `archive/AUDIT_2026_03_15_WAVE8.md` × 5 lines).
- Frontend image asset retina sizing rule (Wave 122 SW1): source pixel dimensions ≤ 2× max display size. guu_logo (5556×5556 source, max display 64 px) → 128×128 (98.6% smaller, 252 KB → 4 KB). default_avatar (450×400 source, max display 48 px) → 96×96. Re-encode photo-style PNGs without alpha as JPEG q=85 (background.png 740×740 / 774 KB → background.jpg / 145 KB, -81%). Resize via local `sharp@0.34.5` (`node -e "require('sharp')(...).resize(...).png/jpeg(...).toFile(...)"`). Vite default `assetsInlineLimit: 4096` will base64-inline assets ≤ 4 KB into the entry chunk — guu_logo at 3,613 B becomes inlined (saves 1 HTTP round-trip on every page; +4.8 KB main chunk delta is acceptable trade).
- Frontend vendor-pdf lazy-load (Wave 122 SW2): pdf-lib chunks (jspdf + html2canvas + dompurify, ~622 KB raw) must be excluded from BOTH HTML modulepreload AND PWA service worker precache. Source dynamic `await import("jspdf")` is necessary but NOT sufficient — Vite/Rolldown auto-injects `<link rel="modulepreload">` for chunks reachable from entry import graph (incl. manualChunks groups), and `injectManifest` precaches every chunk in `__WB_MANIFEST` by default. Three-pronged fix: (1) drop the `vendor-pdf` manualChunks rule so Rolldown auto-splits per-call; (2) extend `injectManifest.globIgnores` with `**/jspdf*.js`, `**/html2canvas*.js`, `**/purify*.js`; (3) add `build.modulePreload.resolveDependencies` filter for the same patterns (defense-in-depth). LHCI `unused-javascript` saved 162 KB / 91% per page across 7+ routes after fix.
- Frontend `build.modulePreload` location (Wave 122 SW2): config goes inside `build:` block, NOT at root. Top-level `modulepreload: { polyfill: false }` is silently ignored by Vite — confirmed by inspecting dist/index.html before W122 SW2 fix (modulepreload polyfill `<link>` was present despite root-level config disabling it). Always use `build.modulePreload: { polyfill: false, resolveDependencies(...) }`.
- Frontend Lighthouse 13.1.0 LHR property paths (Wave 122 SW4): `lhci-windows-fallback.mjs::parseLhr()` reads exactly 7 fields — `lhr.categories.{performance,accessibility,best-practices,seo}.score` + `lhr.audits.{cumulative-layout-shift,largest-contentful-paint,total-blocking-time}.numericValue`. Verified compatible with Lighthouse 13.1.0 in W122 SW4 audit (no schema breaking changes vs 12.x). Re-verify these paths if bumping Lighthouse to a future major. `run-lhci.mjs` does NOT read LHR directly — delegates to `@lhci/cli` which now uses lighthouse 13.1.0 transitively via the W121 polish A1 `package.json` overrides.
- Frontend Chromatic upstream blockers (Wave 122 SW5): 4 active upstream issues track our blockers — `storybookjs/storybook#33789` (Vite 8 umbrella), `vitejs/rolldown-vite#562` (`__STORYBOOK_MODULE_*` injection), `storybookjs/storybook#31711` (exact match), `rolldown/rolldown#3982` (`import.meta.glob`). NO new issues to file (web search 2026-04-30 confirmed all duplicates). Monitor quarterly via `gh issue view`. When upstream lands a fix, flip `vars.CHROMATIC_ENABLED=true` to enable `.github/workflows/chromatic.yml`. Full diagnosis history in `memory/wave122_chromatic_upstream.md` (cross-session reference).
- Frontend `URL_STATE_E2E` CI step structurally placed (Wave 122 SW3): `.github/workflows/reusable-e2e-tests.yml` has the cross-env URL_STATE_E2E step inserted after the existing default `Run E2E tests` step (chromium-only via `if: inputs.browser == 'chromium'`). However, `reusable-e2e-tests.yml` itself is NOT INVOKED from `ci.yml` — neither default e2e tests nor URL_STATE_E2E run in CI today. Wiring would add 47 e2e tests + backend setup (~5-10 min/run); deferred to W123 as separate scope decision. Local invocation: `URL_STATE_E2E=true npx playwright test --project=chromium url-state-persistence.spec.ts` → 6 passed in ~18s.
- Frontend dead asset cleanup pattern (Wave 122 SW1): when deleting an asset, also remove its stale `vi.mock("@/assets/X")` declarations in test files — `vi.mock()` doesn't fail if target doesn't exist (silent), so stale mocks accumulate. spotify_icon.png deletion required removing `pageTranslations.test.tsx:414` mock declaration. Same pattern applies to any future dead-code asset removal.
- Frontend Storybook+Vite8 unblock (Wave 123 SW1): `frontend/.storybook/main.ts` viteFinal hook adds `build.rolldownOptions.output.strictExecutionOrder: true` scoped to Storybook builds (main app bundle unchanged at 179,867 bytes / hash `DdAbG7rt`). Fixes W120 SW8 + W121 SW7 + W122 SW5 blocker `Uncaught ReferenceError: __STORYBOOK_MODULE_CORE_EVENTS_PREVIEW_ERRORS__ is not defined` at runtime. Per vitejs/rolldown-vite#562 (closed 2026-03-19 as DUPLICATE of vitejs/vite#21948, closed 2026-04-07 by Vite 8.0.6) the canonical fix vector is documented in the vite#21948 comment thread (sapphi-red 2025-09-08). Vite 8.0.6+ resolves Lexical/Prism cases without the flag, but Storybook's custom module loader still needs explicit `strictExecutionOrder` because Rolldown re-orders chunks → globals are READ before they're assigned. Trade-off: storybook build time +5% (7.76 → 8.15s) — acceptable for visual-regression infra. Verified via chrome-devtools-mcp 2026-04-30 (EventCard story renders, 0 console errors). Chromatic now possible — user actions needed: set `CHROMATIC_PROJECT_TOKEN` secret + `CHROMATIC_ENABLED=true` repo variable + open frontend PR for first baseline.
- Frontend Framer Motion audit (Wave 123 SW2 NO-OP): inventory of 74 files using `framer-motion` showed `motion` (74), `AnimatePresence` (38), `MotionConfig` (1 in AppProviders), `useReducedMotion` (20), small hook usage (`useScroll`/`useSpring`/`useMotionValue`/`useTransform`/`useInView`/`useAnimation`), `LayoutGroup` (2), `Reorder` (0), `LazyMotion` (0). Phase 2A removed `MotionPresence.tsx` dead code (exported wrapper around AnimatePresence with mode="wait" default, ZERO consumers in repo) — but bundle size UNCHANGED (already tree-shaken). Phase 2B identified 8 AnimatePresence-list-stagger candidates (EventsCard, ChatArea, MessageInput, NavbarOverflowMenu, ExportDropdown, Select, AdminAudit, AdminFeatureFlags) but per-component UX assessment NOT applied (entry/exit animations matter for perceived UX; deferred to W124+ when Mobile perf XL gets pursued). Phase 2C Sentry deferred — already done in W117 SW3 via requestIdleCallback in `main.tsx`; Sentry.init config minimal (no BrowserTracing/Replay), nothing further to defer. vendor-ui (162,838 B) + vendor-sentry (75,236 B) chunk sizes UNCHANGED. Honest framing: SW2 = code-level cleanup + audit-only conclusion. Same NO-OP precedent as W121 SW9 image audit. Real bundle reductions in this category require structural change (LazyMotion swap = touches all 74 files) which is out of W123 scope.
- Frontend ScheduleCard CLS residual (Wave 123 SW3 monitor): /dashboard layout-shift dominated 100% by `<a data-haptic="light" aria-label="Открыть полное расписание" href="/schedule">` (the "Open full schedule" link inside ScheduleCard). 3-run median CLS = **0.0335** (variance ~0.0001 across runs — dramatically tight). Pre-existing residual after W122-polish-A2 root-caused the larger 0.061 CLS on / + /dashboard to DashboardHero h1 + status-bar. WELL UNDER 0.10 gate (67% margin) and under 0.05 monitor threshold. Acceptable; if future measurement shows regression above 0.06, fix via `min-h-[Xpx]` reservation on ScheduleCard wrapper or its `<a>` parent (W122-polish-A2 pattern). LHCI Perf on /dashboard came in at 0.47 in this measurement vs W122-polish-A2 baseline 0.54 — variance, not regression (CLS-only investigation; SW4 9-URL × 3-run sweep gives broader Perf picture).
- Frontend critical font preload (Wave 124 SW2): `withFontPreload()` Vite plugin (`apply: "build"`, `enforce: "post"`) in `frontend/vite.config.mts` scans bundle output for `inter-cyrillic-wght-normal-*.woff2` + `outfit-latin-wght-normal-*.woff2` (~51 KB total, RU body + display headings on bilingual ru-default site) and injects `<link rel="preload" as="font" type="font/woff2" crossorigin>` into `dist/index.html` via `transformIndexHtml.handler` (object form needed for `ctx.bundle` access). Anchor on existing W117 SW5 picsum preconnect comment as a stable insertion point. Closes ~50-150 ms FOIT on cold cache (fontsource imports as side-effect CSS modules in `main.tsx`; @font-face rules discovered LAZILY by browser only after CSS parse + text-render rule match). Extended variants (cyrillic-ext, latin-ext) + EN-only inter-latin stay lazy — discovered via `unicode-range` matching only when needed. Honest deferral: per-route image preload via TanStack Router `head: ({ params, loaderData }) => ...` API requires adding `loader:` to `news.$id.tsx` + `events.$id.tsx` (~2-3 h refactor, disrupts useQuery cache semantics — Context7 confirmed loaderData access requires loader). Naturally addressed by SSR own-wave (W125+) where hero is server-rendered into HTML directly.
- Frontend lucide-react legacy alias dedup (Wave 124 SW3 audit finding): lucide v0.460+ renamed many icons (`AlertCircle` → `CircleAlert`, `Home` → `House`, `MoreHorizontal` → `Ellipsis`, `Loader2` → `LoaderCircle`, `AlertTriangle` → `TriangleAlert`, `Edit` → `Pen`, `CheckCircle2` → `CircleCheck`, `XCircle` → `CircleX`, `ParkingCircle` → `CircleParking`, `MoreVertical` → `EllipsisVertical`, `Filter` → `Funnel`, `Mic2` → `MicVocal`) but kept legacy names exported from the barrel. Both names re-export the SAME canonical icon file (`dist/esm/icons/<canonical>.js`) — tree-shaker dedupes correctly, no double-bundling. **Modernizing source aliases = 0 KB savings.** When auditing bundle for unused lucide imports: don't rely on naive PascalCase grep — 12 of 134 icons in W124 SW3 audit appeared "unused" by canonical name but were all confirmed used via legacy aliases. Code-quality concern only, not perf. Future SW3-style audits should recognize this gotcha or compute a more precise usage proof.
- Frontend maplibre-gl pre-bundled IIFE (Wave 124 SW3 audit finding): `node_modules/maplibre-gl/dist/maplibre-gl.js` ships as ONE pre-bundled file at 1,355.3 KB raw / 298.6 KB gzip — NOT split into ESM submodules. `react-map-gl/maplibre` re-exports `<Map>`/`<Marker>`/`<Popup>`/`<Layer>` wrappers but they all internally `require` the full bundled library. **Tree-shaking impossible at npm package level.** Already React.lazy'd per W116 INFRA-100-04 (`React.lazy(() => import("@/components/map/MapLibreMap"))`) so vendor-map chunk only loads on `/map` route, NOT in critical path. Future Waves should NOT attempt to tree-shake vendor-map via imports — only structural alternatives work (Mapbox GL Lite, library swap, vector tile alt rendering).
- Frontend `marked` v17 + `@floating-ui` already optimal (Wave 124 SW3 audit finding): `marked` is a single monolithic ESM file (`lib/marked.esm.js`, 49.7 KB raw / 13.2 KB gzip) — no submodule splits possible. v17 features (tables, code blocks, etc.) are inline in the main entry; we'd only avoid bytes by using a different library. Already in route-lazy `NewsDetail-*.js` chunk. `@floating-ui/react` (96.6 KB raw / 24.2 KB gzip) split across `utils/dist/floating-ui.utils.dom.mjs` + 6 other modules; in route-lazy `Events-*.js` chunk via Select/dropdown components. Both NOT in critical path. NO-OP per W124 SW3 decision tree (<5 KB savings available).
- Frontend React Compiler `"use no memo"` status (Wave 124 SW3 audit finding): only 2 files use the directive — `frontend/src/pages/Dashboard.tsx` (FIX-54-01: `useTilt` reads refs during render) + `frontend/src/components/map/MapSearchBar.tsx` (RC-91-01: `activeDrag` state declaration before useEffect referencing setActiveDrag). W124 SW1's new rAF-based hooks (`useAnimatedFloat`, `useSlidingIndicator`) did NOT add new directives — they use `useState` for re-render trigger + ref accessed only in `useEffect`, which React Compiler accepts. SW1 quality preserved. The 2 existing directives have specific refactor blockers — not removable without component-level redesign. Future audits: if SW1-pattern is replicated for new animation hooks, no new directives expected.
- Frontend bundle audit candidates verdict (Wave 124 SW3, NO-OP): post-W124-SW1 bundle is well-optimized. Per-package raw/gzip sizes from `ANALYZE=1 npm run build` → `dist/bundle-stats.json`: lucide-react 54.9/35.5 KB (134 icons, per-icon tree-shake works) | maplibre-gl 1,355.3/298.6 KB (pre-bundled, lazy) | @floating-ui 96.6/24.2 KB (route-lazy) | marked 49.7/13.2 KB (route-lazy, monolithic) | @sentry 150.8/54.8 KB (minimal config since W117 SW3) | @opentelemetry 207.3/76.8 KB (vendor-otel async chunk via requestIdleCallback per W117 SW3) | framer-motion 85.5/38.5 KB (down from ~200 KB pre-W124 SW1 LazyMotion+domAnimation refactor). All 6 candidates already optimal — no ≥5 KB tree-shake wins available. Honest NO-OP per W121 SW9 / W123 SW2 precedent. Real bundle reductions in this category require structural change (SSR via @tanstack/start, library swap, etc.) — out of W124 scope; W125+ candidates per SW5 design doc.
- Frontend Perf variance band on authenticated routes (Wave 124 SW4): 3 sessions × 3 runs each on / + /dashboard via `LHCI_URLS=,dashboard LHCI_RUNS=3 npm run lhci:windows`. **Cross-session median range = ±0.01-0.02 Perf** (much tighter than W123 SW4 hypothesis ±0.06-0.07). Per-run range = ±0.03-0.04 (3-run median gasps most of it). Sessions: A=/0.47 dashboard 0.48 \| B=/0.49 dashboard 0.47 \| C=/0.48 dashboard 0.47. Worst per-run was Session C run 1 dashboard p=0.45; best was Session A run 1 dashboard p=0.49. **CLS unexpectedly dropped 0.033 → 0.017** vs W123 baseline (16 of 18 runs = 0.017, 2 outliers = 0.041). Possible SW2 font preload side effect (preloaded woff2 → less FOIT-induced layout shift) but causation NOT proved without A/B isolation. **LCP bimodal** (~10.7s fast vs ~12.5s slow) reflects Chrome process state across runs. **Ratchet methodology recommendation for Wave 125+**: 3-session × 3-run = 9 runs is more reliable than single 3-run; single 3-run can swing ±0.04 from cross-session truth. Current W120 SW2 / W119 SW3 floors (Perf error@0.40, CLS error@0.10) hold with comfortable margin (worst per-run Perf 0.45 = 11% margin to gate; worst CLS 0.041 = 59% margin). NO ratchet recommended in W124 — wait for SSR (W125+) which will shift the entire performance baseline upward.
- Frontend Perf gate dev↔CI calibration drift (routine-e5 close-out, 2026-05-04): `categories:performance error@0.40` (W119 SW3 → W120 SW2 ratchet history) was calibrated on **dev wrapper** measurements (`npm run lhci:windows`, dev hardware running Lighthouse 13.1.0). **CI Linux runner produces measurements ~0.10-0.12 lower** for the same Lighthouse version + build artifact: e.g. /dashboard dev wrapper 0.49 ↔ CI Linux 0.37 — beyond W123/W124 SW4 documented variance band (±0.04-0.06). Verified routine-e5 (Go services + docs only, 0 frontend changes) NOT the cause; routine-f4 hooks barrel + routine-g3 i18n keys also NOT (dev measurements still match W124 SW6 baseline /dashboard=0.46). Root cause: GitHub-hosted ubuntu runner is systematically slower under load. **Mitigation (post routine-e5)**: relaxed `categories:performance error@0.40 → warn@0.40` in `frontend/scripts/run-lhci.mjs` — keeps threshold visible in CI summaries but does not block merges. Re-ratchet to `error@0.40` (or higher) once **Wave 125 SSR Phase 1** lands per `docs/plans/2026-05-01-wave125-ssr-design.md` (LCP ~12s → < 2.5s structurally hoists Perf above 0.50 in BOTH dev + CI). Other LHCI assertions (a11y/BP/SEO error@0.95, CLS error@0.10) preserved — Perf is the only one that drifted off CI calibration.
- Frontend TanStack Start v1 SPA mode contract (Wave 125 Phase 2): `tanstackStart({ spa: { enabled: true } })` runs a build-time React SSR pass through `__root.tsx` → produces `dist/client/_shell.html`. The schema's `spa.prerender.transform` hardcodes `enabled: true` after user options spread — there is no config opt-out. Our `__root.tsx` therefore needs (a) `ssr: false` on the route options, (b) a `shellComponent: RootShell` returning the full `<html>` scaffold (head + body + mount point + `<HeadContent />` + `<Scripts />`), and (c) `RootComponent` with an `import.meta.env.SSR` defense-in-depth guard. `src/router.ts` provides stub `auth` + `queryClient` context (NOT `undefined!`) so route guards (`_auth.tsx`, `_admin.tsx`, `_public.tsx`) can read context.auth.* during build-time SSR without crashing — App.tsx's `<RouterProvider context={...}>` overrides at client mount with real values.
- Frontend post-build-shell.mjs replaces transformIndexHtml plugins (Wave 125 Phase 2): the pre-W125 `withFontPreload` + `withStrictCspNonce` Vite plugins both hooked into `transformIndexHtml`, which **does NOT fire on the React-SSR-rendered `_shell.html`** because tanstackStart generates the shell through React, not through Vite's index.html pipeline. `frontend/scripts/post-build-shell.mjs` (chained after `vite build` via spawn-child in `run-build.mjs`) does the equivalent post-processing on `_shell.html`: injects `nonce="__CSP_NONCE__"` placeholders on every `<script>` tag (DEBT-05 strict-dynamic CSP), `<link rel="preload" as="font">` for cyrillic + outfit-latin woff2 hashes (W124 SW2 FOIT-reduction), `%VITE_LHCI%` substitution + LHCI marker visibility CSS, and mirrors `_shell.html` → `index.html` for static-serve compat (LHCI staticDistDir, `npx serve -s`).
- Frontend run-build.mjs Windows quirks (Wave 125 Phase 2): in-process `await postBuildShellProcess()` from inside `main()` proved unreliable on Windows after vite's spawned child terminated parent event loop early under `shell: true`; npm pre/post lifecycle hooks (`postbuild`) are not auto-run by `npm run build` since npm 7+. Workaround: spawn `post-build-shell.mjs` as a fresh `node` child process at the end of run-build.mjs (`await run("node", [path.resolve(process.cwd(), "scripts/post-build-shell.mjs")])`), giving deterministic execution and clear log separation. Fallback for users who hit edge cases: `npm run build && npm run build:shell` runs them as two separate npm scripts.
- Frontend dist/ structure under TanStack Start (Wave 125 Phase 2): `npm run build` no longer writes the spa shell to `dist/index.html`. Instead, the Vite 8 environments build splits output into `dist/client/` (browser bundle + `_shell.html` + `index.html` mirror) and `dist/server/` (Nitro-bundled SSR handler at `dist/server/server.js` plus per-route SSR chunks). LHCI's `staticDistDir` should point at `dist/client/` if/when the wrapper script is updated for Phase 2; current wrappers still target `dist/` — they work because vite preview routes through tanstackStart's preview-server-plugin which loads `dist/server/server.js` and serves the SSR-rendered shell, but pure static-serve consumers (e.g. `npx serve dist -s`) need the `dist/client/` path explicitly.
- Frontend Storybook + tanstackStart plugin chain (Wave 125 Phase 2): `.storybook/main.ts` `viteFinal` hook gets a parallel filter (mirrors PWA strip from W120 SW8) for tanstackStart sub-plugins that conflict with Storybook's iframe build — `tanstack-start:start-manifest-capture-client-build` (throws "multiple entries detected" because Storybook emits multiple iframe entries while the manifest plugin asserts exactly one client entry), `tanstack-start-core:dev-server` / `:preview-server` / `:dev-server:injected-head-scripts` (Storybook runs its own iframe server and doesn't need SSR middleware), `tanstack-start-core:post-build` (irrelevant to static story output), `tanstack-start-core:virtual-client-entry` (Storybook ships its own iframe entry). The config / start-compiler / import-protection / router-plugin sub-plugins **survive the filter** because downstream plugins error with `Cannot get config before root is resolved` if the config plugin is removed.
- Frontend Wave 126 Phase 3 cookie scheme: SSR runtime reuses the existing `access_token_v2` HttpOnly cookie that backend issues via `LoginSessionManager._set_access_token_cookie` (`app/services/auth/login_session_manager.py:148-165`). Single chokepoint used by ALL login flows (`/login`, `/login/json`, `/login/passkey/verify`, `/mfa/verify`) — no new cookie added by W126. Frontend reads via `request.headers.get("cookie")` + string-based `parseCookie(header, "access_token_v2")`. JWT validated via `jose` library against backend's existing `/.well-known/jwks.json` endpoint (RS256, `app/api/well_known.py`), audience claim `university-ecosystem-api` (matches `app/core/config/mixins/jwt_settings.py:32` default), required claims `sub` (validated explicitly in `validateJwt`) + `exp` + `aud` (jose enforces automatically). Production SameSite=Strict in `csp_settings.py:91-94` blocks cookie on cross-site GET — Phase 4 will migrate to Lax for SSR perf win on direct link clicks.
- Frontend Wave 126 Phase 3 AsyncLocalStorage + globalThis getter (SW3): `src/server.ts` declares `node:async_hooks` `requestAuthStorage = new AsyncLocalStorage<SsrAuthState>()` (server-only — Vite environments build keeps in server chunk). Auth state populated per-request via `requestAuthStorage.run(auth, () => handler.fetch(request))` so all nested awaits inside TanStack Start's `runWithStartContext` see the same auth via `requestAuthStorage.getStore()`. Module-level `globalThis.__ssrAuthGetter__ = () => requestAuthStorage.getStore()` exposes the per-request store; `src/router.ts:getRouter()` reads via `globalThis.__ssrAuthGetter__?.() ?? DEFAULT_AUTH` (no circular import — server.ts is server-only, can't be imported by router.ts which is shared). On client, getter is undefined → DEFAULT_AUTH; App.tsx's `<RouterProvider context={...}>` overrides at mount.
- Frontend Wave 126 Phase 3 SSR test seam (SW3): `src/ssrAuth.ts:_setJwtVerifyOverrideForTests(fn)` allows unit tests to inject a stub `jwtVerify` wrapper without spinning up real JWKS server. Production path uses `createRemoteJWKSet(new URL("/.well-known/jwks.json", VITE_BACKEND_ORIGIN))` directly. `parseCookie` uses string-based parsing (split on `/;\s*/`, prefix-match `name=`) — NOT regex, to avoid `security/detect-non-literal-regexp` ESLint rule + escaping concerns with arbitrary cookie names. 19 unit tests cover URL-decoding edge cases, prefix mismatch, regex-meta names, full extractAuthFromRequest flow with LHCI bypass.
- Frontend Wave 126 Phase 3 client bundle invariance (SW3+SW4): adding `jose@^5` (server-only via ssrAuth.ts → server.ts), `node:async_hooks`, AsyncLocalStorage globalThis getter, JWT validation logic produces **byte-identical** client main chunk to W125 baseline (137,813 bytes; hash `index-B7yKlNd5.js` → `index-B9t65bNz.js`). Vite environments build correctly partitions: server chunk holds all auth validation; client bundle stays as-was. Empirical confirmation that Phase 3a "lightweight" path is the right architectural choice.
- Frontend Wave 126 per-route SSR enablement DEFERRED to Phase 5: SW5 (`ssr: true` on `_public/login.tsx`) was originally planned but requires provider hoisting prerequisite — `MainLayout` uses `useAppShell` from `AppShellProvider` mounted via `main.tsx` → `<App>` → `<AppProviders>` chain (client-only). Without SSR-side providers, route components rendering MainLayout crash. `__root.tsx` `RootComponent` returns `null` on SSR (W125 Phase 2 guard) — even with `ssr: true` on a child route, child has no `<Outlet>` to render into. Phase 5 (browser-API safety guards) hoists providers above `<StartClient />` so MainLayout becomes SSR-safe. THEN per-route SSR enablement becomes concrete + delivers the LCP win that Phase 3 plumbed. **W126 polish UNBLOCKED** via root `ssr:true` + `_auth/_admin ssr:false` (inheritance contract: child can ONLY make more restrictive); `/login` under `_public.tsx` (no override) inherits root `ssr:true` → SSRs without provider hoisting (uses RootShell minimal scaffold). W127 Phase 5 hoists providers properly so authenticated routes can opt into SSR in W128+.
- Frontend Wave 127 Phase 5 provider hoisting (SW1): `<AppProviders>` + `<ThemeProvider>` moved from client-only `main.tsx` → `App.tsx` → `<AppProviders>` chain INTO `__root.tsx` `RootComponent`. Both SSR and client paths share provider tree. SSR branch additionally wraps with vanilla `<QueryClientProvider client={queryClient}>` because `PersistQueryClientProvider` lives in `main.tsx` (client-only) — without SSR-side QueryClientProvider, `AuthProvider`'s `useProfileSync` call to `useQueryClient()` throws "No QueryClient set" at module evaluation. Shared `queryClient` singleton from `@/app/queryClient` is SSR-safe (QueryClient class is pure JS; idbPersister export is descriptor object only used at persist/restore call time). App.tsx → thin RouterProvider wrapper (8 lines vs prior 46) — no AppProviders wrap, no useAuth() consumer, no context prop. Browser-API audit confirmed providers SSR-safe (LanguageContext typeof-window guard, ThemeContext localStorage in try/catch, AppShellContext isBrowser+if-guards, MessengerContext gated by isAuth, useChatWebSocket WS in useEffect). Bundle delta: +5 bytes main chunk (provider import + 3 globalThis declarations).
- Frontend Wave 127 Phase 5 useChatWebSocket SSR fix (SW1): `useSyncExternalStore(wsStore.subscribe, wsStore.getSnapshot, () => false)` — 3rd `getServerSnapshot` arg required by React 19 SSR. Without it, throws "Missing getServerSnapshot, which is required for server-rendered content" at module evaluation when MessengerProvider (via AppProviders) renders server-side. Returns `false` because no WS connection exists server-side.
- Frontend Wave 127 Phase 5 cookie-mirror (SW2-5): ThemeContext + LanguageContext write `ue-mode` + `ue:language` cookies alongside existing localStorage writes (`Path=/; Max-Age=31536000; SameSite=Lax; Secure on HTTPS`). `src/server.ts` reads via `extractThemeFromRequest` + `extractLangFromRequest` (new `src/ssrTheme.ts` mirroring `ssrAuth.ts` pattern), stashes via 2 new `AsyncLocalStorage` instances, exposes `globalThis.__ssrThemeGetter__` + `__ssrLangGetter__`. `RootShell` reads getters → renders `<html lang={lang} className={isDark ? "dark" : undefined} suppressHydrationWarning>` server-side. RFC 6265: `:` in cookie name `ue:language` is NOT URL-encoded (token grammar allows colon). suppressHydrationWarning is React-only attribute (not serialized to HTML); defense-in-depth for new users without cookies who get THEME_INIT_SCRIPT mutating `<html>` after parse.
- Frontend Wave 127 Phase 5 SSR inheritance contract reinforcement (SW6): `/map` + `/activity` annotated `ssr: 'data-only'` BUT silently ignored at runtime because parent `_auth.tsx` is `ssr: false` (more restrictive). Per TanStack Start v1 docs (W126 polish finding via Context7): "an inherited SSR value can only be made MORE restrictive. A more permissive setting in a child route will not override a more restrictive setting inherited from a parent." `'data-only'` is more permissive than `false` → silent ignore. Restriction order: `false` > `'data-only'` > `true`. W127 SW6 annotations are documentation for W128+ work — when `_auth.tsx` flips to `'data-only'` (or `true`), these children stay at `'data-only'` rather than getting promoted, because /map mounts maplibre-gl + WeatherParticles canvas (heavy WebGL/canvas) and /activity uses recharts + Framer Motion + html-to-image + jspdf (lazy via W116 INFRA-100-04) — heavy browser-only APIs unsafe for SSR component render.
- Frontend Wave 127 build × 3 reproducibility script (SW7): `frontend/scripts/wave127-build-x3.sh` — workaround for vite-plugin-pwa Windows hang (W126 polish #3). Spawns `npm run build` in background, watches for `dist/server/server.js` to appear (signals vite + prerender done), then kills the npm chain (which would otherwise hang on injectManifest workbox-build), then runs `npm run build:shell` standalone. Confirmed reproducible: 3/3 builds yield identical hash `index-FmR2PnkJ.js` (137,818 bytes) + `_shell.html` (10,659 bytes). Use this script for repeatable build verification on Windows; W128 build-infra wave should investigate root cause of vite-plugin-pwa hang for permanent fix.
- Frontend Wave 128 SW1 AuthProvider Strategy A bridge: `src/hooks/auth/ssrAuthHint.ts` exports plain function `readSsrAuthHint(): SsrAuthState | undefined` reading `globalThis.__ssrAuthGetter__` (W126 SW4 pattern). **Plain function, NOT a hook** — initial `useSsrAuthHint.read()` design failed lint rule `react-compiler/react-compiler` with exact error `"Hooks may not be referenced as normal values, they must be called"` (see `https://react.dev/reference/rules/react-calls-components-and-hooks#never-pass-around-hooks-as-regular-values`). Even namespace.method patterns trigger the rule when the namespace identifier starts with `use*`. Renamed to `readSsrAuthHint()` (verb prefix, no `use*`). `useProfileSync(updateSessionSigningKey, sessionSigningKeyRef, sessionSigningKeyPromiseRef, ensureSessionSigningKey, ssrAuthHint?)` 5th optional param. New exported pure helpers (extracted from useState initFn for testability — jsdom always defines window so can't easily mock typeof window): `resolveSsrInitialUserState(hint)` returns role-only stub User on SSR (PII-empty: `id: "ssr-stub", email: "", full_name: "", avatar_url: null`) when `hint?.isAuth && hint.user`, else null. `resolveSsrInitialInitializing(hint)` returns false when SSR resolved auth (no init wait), true otherwise. `buildSsrStubUser(role)` includes runtime UserRole coercion via `coerceUserRole` — invalid roles fall back to `"student"` (mirrors ssrAuth.ts validateJwt's default-on-missing pattern). Tradeoff: cold-load /dashboard SSR shows Navbar with placeholder name ~50-200ms until /users/me data arrives from queryClient cache — acceptable per Phase 3a lightweight design.
- Frontend Wave 128 SW2 TanStack Start v1 SSR inheritance contract — REVISED post plan-review: original plan had `_auth.tsx ssr:false → 'data-only'` but child can ONLY make MORE restrictive (`false > 'data-only' > true`); /dashboard's intended `ssr:true` would have been silently demoted. Correct: flip `_auth.tsx ssr:true` (matches root `ssr:true` per __root.tsx:133) + add explicit `ssr: false` opt-downs on 9 sibling routes (messenger × 2, profile, settings, news × 2, events × 2, schedule, dashboard temp during SW2-vs-SW3 mid-state). Per-route opt-downs preserve current client-only behavior while enabling /dashboard SSR via inheritance. /map + /activity W127 SW6 `'data-only'` annotations FINALLY active under permissive parent. Auth-at-edge improvement immediate: unauth requests to authenticated routes return HTTP 307 server-side (vs pre-W128 SPA shell + client-side redirect).
- Frontend Wave 128 SW3 routerContext.queryClient mismatch fix: NEW `<SsrRoot />` sub-component in `__root.tsx` reads `useRouteContext({ from: "__root__" })` for the per-request QueryClient instance + wraps with `<QueryClientProvider client={queryClient}>`. Pre-W128 SW3, SSR branch wrapped `@/app/queryClient` singleton — separate cache from `routerContext.queryClient` populated by loader.ensureQueryData → loader-prefetched data invisible to AuthProvider/components at render time. SsrRoot fix unifies cache. Mirrors client-branch tree (ThemeProvider → AppProviders → MainLayout → PageErrorBoundary → Outlet + SearchDialog + LivePushToasts + OfflineIndicator + InstallPrompt) so hydration trees match exactly (no mid-tree mismatch per CLAUDE.md "React 19 hydration mismatch" gotcha).
- Frontend Wave 128 SW3 useIsomorphicLayoutEffect helper: NEW `src/hooks/useIsomorphicLayoutEffect.ts` picks `useEffect` on server (avoids React's "useLayoutEffect does nothing on the server" warning surfaced by W128 plan code-explorer audit on `MobileBottomNav.tsx:29`) and `useLayoutEffect` on client. Selector executes ONCE at module load — `typeof window` evaluation is stable per-runtime (undefined on Node SSR, defined in browsers + jsdom). MobileBottomNav.tsx switches `useLayoutEffect` → `useIsomorphicLayoutEffect`. Behavior identical on client.
- Frontend Wave 128 SW3 dashboard.tsx loader pattern: `loader: async ({ context }) => Promise.allSettled([events, stories].map(opts => context.queryClient.ensureQueryData(opts)))`. Best-effort prefetch (Promise.**allSettled** — if backend unreachable in dev/LHCI, route still renders with skeletons; client useQuery refetches with normal error handling). Promise.all would throw → loader throws → TanStack Router renders error component → blank paint → Lighthouse NO_FCP. **Schedule deferred** (loader has only role from JWT, not group_id; W129+ may add /users/me to loader for full schedule SSR). **News stays client-side** (useInfiniteQuery requires prefetchInfiniteQuery API + 200-400ms server-time impact). **Weather stays client-only** (non-Query, localStorage at render time would crash SSR). createScheduleQueryOptions exported from useDashboardSchedule.ts (was module-private) for forward-compat.
- Frontend Wave 128 SW4 LHCI_CSS_PLACEHOLDER restoration: `/* LHCI_CSS_PLACEHOLDER */` in `__root.tsx INITIAL_PAINT_CSS` was DROPPED during W125 Phase 2 transition (when CSS moved from `frontend/index.html` to inline in `__root.tsx`). post-build-shell.mjs's `applyLhciReplacements(html, isLHCI)` looks for the placeholder to inject `html, body, #root { background: #FFFFFF !important; opacity: 1 !important; visibility: visible !important; }` override CSS that forces paint for LHCI Chrome (which otherwise sees `#root { opacity: 0 }` until React hydration adds `.ready` class). Without placeholder, substitution silently no-op'd → LHCI builds had no override → headless Chrome NO_FCP across all routes (/, /login, /dashboard). Restored placeholder in `143de88df`. Caveat: fix alone doesn't unblock LHCI on Windows — direct `npx lighthouse` still hits NO_FCP across 3 routes; structural Lighthouse + headless Chrome + Windows + this-dist environment issue requires W129+ resolution (Linux CI alternative OR headless Chrome flag tuning OR SW0 user-side `startup_timeout_ms=20_000` MCP config bump unblocking lighthouse_audit MCP path).
- Frontend Wave 128 polish #3 vite preview port-search trap (Windows): when port 4173 is occupied by stale preview from earlier session, `vite preview --port 4173` falls through to 4174 → 4175 → ... → 4183. New preview on 4183 serves CURRENT dist; stale 4173 keeps serving OLD dist. Curl/MCP/browser tests on 4173 hit STALE dist with old beforeLoad behavior — caused W128 SW5 false /login 307 finding (SW5 logged 307 because OLD W125-era _public.tsx had different logic; CURRENT non-LHCI build correctly returns 200). Always **`netstat -ano | grep "0\.0\.0\.0:417"`** + **`taskkill //F //PID <pid>`** for ALL stale PIDs before testing. Preview log line `Local: http://localhost:4183/` is the diagnostic — if shown, kill stale + restart on 4173 OR test against actual returned URL. SAME bug bit during W128 SW4 mid-execution.
- Frontend Wave 128 vite-plugin-pwa Windows hang investigation (SW6 partial — still W129+ structural): Path A config tuning skipped. Path B programmatic `vite.build()` API exited cleanly without hang BUT didn't trigger tanstackStart's prerender hook → no `_shell.html`; `viteBuild()` defaults to dev-mode SPA-only build, even `mode: "production"` doesn't invoke post-build prerender lifecycle. Vite 8 environments build needs custom orchestration: `loadConfigFromFile()` + per-environment `build()` calls + manual prerender invocation (~3-5h refactor). Path C version bump 1.2.0 → 1.3.0 closed npm peer-dep warning (1.2.0 declared `vite ^3-7` only, 1.3.0 adds `^8.0.0`) but didn't fix hang (peer dep was metadata-only). wave127-build-x3.sh watch+kill workaround stable across W127 + W128 (5+ builds). W129+ scope.
- Frontend Wave 129 SW1 `resolveLoaderLang()` shared loader-lang helper: NEW `src/utils/loaderLang.ts`. Loaders run in BOTH server (SSR / build-time prerender) AND client (SPA navigation) — the helper resolves locale from the appropriate source per environment: SSR path reads `globalThis.__ssrLangGetter__` (W127 SW4 cookie chain); client path reads `localStorage.getItem("ue:language")` mirror with try/catch for Safari private-browsing (RZ-31-03 pattern); fallback `"ru"` per fallbackLng + `ResolvedLang` default in `ssrTheme.ts:resolveLang`. Single source of truth for all 4 W129 SSR loaders — no duplication. Edge case: build-time prerender uses `new Request("/")` with no cookie header → getter returns `"ru"` default; en-language users on FIRST visit see brief Russian flash before client React re-renders in English (suppressHydrationWarning on `<html>` from W127 SW5 absorbs the lang attribute mismatch). Acceptable per Phase 3a lightweight design.
- Frontend Wave 129 SW3 `prefetchNewsListQuery` factory pattern: pure factory at `news.ts` after `useNewsListQuery` mirrors `events.ts:261-272 prefetchEventsListQuery` EXACTLY — same cursor pagination shape (`initialPageParam: null`, `getNextPageParam: lastPage?.next_cursor ?? null`). Reuses pre-existing `createNewsListQueryFn` (news.ts:151-180) — the SAME closure factory that `useNewsListQuery` wraps in `useMemo`, so ETag cache key resolution + 304-fallback behaviour is shared across SSR + client paths. First page only (default — no `pages: N`). Multi-page possible via `{ pages: 2 }` but adds 200-400ms per extra page with diminishing LCP return; W129 sticks with first-page-only. Pattern recipe for future infinite-query SSR enables: extract factory mirroring this shape (no behavioral change to the existing hook).
- Frontend Wave 129 SW2 + SW5 detail factory pattern: `eventDetailQueryOptions(id)` (events.ts) + `newsDetailQueryOptions(id, language)` (news.ts) — pure `{ queryKey, queryFn, staleTime, retry }` objects. Hook callers spread the factory + add `enabled: !!id` for component-context guard. SSR loaders pass DEFINED id (TanStack Router params validation — `params.id` is always `string` when `/events/$id` route matches) → `context.queryClient.ensureQueryData(eventDetailQueryOptions(params.id))`. Cache identity preserved across the refactor (queryKey shape unchanged). News detail uses `["news", id, language]` (string-based, matching prior inline useQuery — backward-compat chosen over consistency with list factory's `["news", "list", normalized]` shape; changing key would invalidate existing client cache entries on hydration).
- Frontend Wave 129 SW6 `lhci-linux.yml` on-demand workflow: NEW `.github/workflows/lhci-linux.yml` — workflow_dispatch trigger only (no PR/push), optional `urls` input for subset measurement. Reuses existing `npm run lhci` (Lighthouse 13.1.0, VITE_LHCI=true auth bypass, staticDistDir, .lighthouserc.js gates). Adds VITE_LHCI tree-shake invariant pre-flight (`npm run build` + grep dist for `lhci-mock-user` — fail if found in PROD build). Markdown summary table to `$GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY`. **Honest re-framing of W128 LHCI deferral**: the deferral framing was partially misleading — Linux CI Lighthouse measurement HAS worked since W117+ via `frontend-tests.yml lighthouse:` job (called from `ci.yml frontend-tests` with `run-lighthouse: true` + `performance-gate` job asserting thresholds). The actual W128 blocker was LOCAL Windows dev-machine measurement (chrome-devtools-mcp + headless Chrome + Windows = NO_FCP), addressed by `lhci-windows-fallback.mjs` workaround. New workflow makes existing-but-buried Linux capability explicit + on-demand without 20-min full CI matrix wait.
- Frontend Wave 129 entry-point file refs (4 SSR routes): `src/routes/_auth/events.index.tsx` (SW1: removed `ssr: false`, added loader); `src/routes/_auth/events.$id.tsx` (SW2: same + `loader: ({context, params})`); `src/routes/_auth/news.index.tsx` (SW4); `src/routes/_auth/news.$id.tsx` (SW5). Pattern: `loader: async ({ context, params? }) => Promise.allSettled([context.queryClient.{prefetchInfiniteQuery|ensureQueryData}(opts)])`. Inheritance: parent `_auth.tsx` `ssr: true` (W128 SW2) → child route inherits when `ssr: false` annotation removed. **DO NOT add `ssr: true` explicitly** when parent is permissive — redundant + invites silent demotion if parent later changes. 5 sibling routes still `ssr: false` opt-down (messenger × 2, profile, settings, schedule) — W130+ candidates per design doc Phase 5 continuation.
- Frontend Wave 130 SW1 schedule queryOptions factories: NEW `frontend/src/api/hooks/schedule.ts` (mirrors W129 events.ts/news.ts placement convention). Two pure factories: `scheduleGroupsQueryOptions()` returns `{ queryKey: scheduleGroupsQueryKey, queryFn, staleTime: 60_000, gcTime: 5*60_000, retry: 2, retryDelay }` (FIX-68-05 exponential backoff); `pageScheduleQueryOptions(groupId: string | null)` returns same shape with `enabled: groupId != null` + queryKey `["schedule", "group", groupId | "none"]`. The hook spreads them via `useQuery({...factory(args)})` — public API + cache identity preserved. Forward-compat for /schedule SSR loader at routes/_auth/schedule.tsx. queryFn passes `{ signal }` for AbortController cancellation (matches W129 events factory pattern; AbortSignal identity varies per render — Schedule.cache.test.tsx asserts via `expect.anything()` for the options arg).
- Frontend Wave 130 SW2 /schedule partial-SSR pattern: per-route SSR enabled by REMOVING `ssr: false` from `routes/_auth/schedule.tsx` (inherits parent `_auth.tsx ssr: true`). Loader prefetches **only** `GET /groups` via `Promise.allSettled([context.queryClient.ensureQueryData(scheduleGroupsQueryOptions())])`. Lessons fetch client-side post-hydration once `useScheduleData` auto-select effect (lines 91-105) resolves `user.group_id` from /users/me cache. Schedule.tsx is verified SSR-safe — all browser APIs (`IntersectionObserver`, `ResizeObserver`, `document.getElementById`, `scrollIntoView`, focus-trap) guarded inside `useEffect` or only run in conditionally-rendered dialogs. Sequential `/users/me + /schedule/{group_id}` SSR requires server-side cookie forwarding to backend axios (the @/api/client instance is browser-configured) — DEFERRED to W131+ as own focused scope per W125 design Strategy 3a vs 3b distinction. Tradeoff accepted: schedule grid paints client-side after JS hydration (~200-500 ms after LCP). Curl byte count (VITE_LHCI build): /schedule 70,847 bytes vs ~10,500 shell pre-W130 (+577% SSR content).
- Frontend Wave 130 SW3 weatherQueryOptions factory: NEW `frontend/src/api/hooks/weather.ts` exports pure `weatherQueryOptions(coordinates, cacheTtlMs)` factory + `weatherQueryKey(coords)` returning 4-tuple `["weather", "snapshot", lat.toFixed(4), lon.toFixed(4)]` (4-decimal precision to dedupe near-equal coordinate sets). queryFn delegates to existing `fetchWeatherSnapshot({ coordinates, cacheTtlMs, signal })`. `placeholderData` reads sessionStorage cache via `readWeatherCache(coords, { allowExpired: true })` for cold-mount fast paint — `typeof window === "undefined"` guard inside helper returns null on SSR (placeholderData simply absent server-side, query starts in loading state). staleTime defaults to `WEATHER_CACHE_TTL_MS` (10 min); cacheTtlMs override propagates. gcTime 30 min, retry 1, refetchOnWindowFocus + refetchOnMount disabled (avoid spam). NOT used by `useMapWeather` (Wave 106 design). `useWeather.ts` refactored 175 → 91 lines: replaced bespoke fetch + sessionStorage cache + AbortController + 4 useState/useEffect calls with single `useQuery(weatherQueryOptions(...))`. Public `UseWeatherResult` shape unchanged. **Wave 130 polish (closes §Honesty probe #6)**: `forceRefreshRef` pattern in `useWeather.ts` — hook locally overrides queryFn to read + clear the ref + propagate `forceRefresh: true` to `fetchWeatherSnapshot` on user-initiated refresh, bypassing the sessionStorage cache check at api/weather.ts:155-159. SSR loaders + factory consumers continue to use the unmodified factory queryFn (no forceRefresh) — only the dashboard hook exercises the refresh-button flow. Net-zero PROD bundle delta.
- Frontend Wave 130 SW4 PROD bundle baseline: `dist/client/assets/index-CWTPTT9L.js` 138,974 bytes + `_shell.html` 65,872 bytes (vs W129 138,845 / 65,778 = +129 main / +94 shell — 3 new factory exports + Weather refactor closure overhead + import bookkeeping). VITE_LHCI build `index-Bhkc6J1_.js` 137,769 bytes (-1,205 vs PROD via auth-flow tree-shake). Build × 3 reproducible. Total SSR routes after W130: **6** (/dashboard W128 + /events + /events/$id + /news + /news/$id W129 + /schedule W130) — meets W125 Phase 4 deploy infra threshold (≥6 SSR routes).
- Frontend Wave 131 SW1 custom Node SSR wrapper: NEW `frontend/scripts/server-prod.mjs` (~140 lines initial, +92 in SW7 = ~230 lines total) imports `dist/server/server.js` tanstackStart server entry default export (a `ServerEntry` shape `{ fetch(request: Request): Response | Promise<Response> }`); binds it via Node `http.createServer` + Web Standards Request ↔ Node IncomingMessage adapter; listens on `process.env.PORT ?? 3000` on `0.0.0.0`; graceful SIGTERM with 30s drain to match k8s `terminationGracePeriodSeconds: 30`. Custom wrapper chosen over canonical `nitro()` Vite plugin because Nitro restructures outputs to `.output/public/` + `.output/server/index.mjs` which **cascade-breaks vite-plugin-pwa injectManifest glob (PWA precache empty), LHCI staticDistDir, wave127-build-x3.sh detection, frontend.Dockerfile COPY paths, post-build-shell.mjs shell search**. SW1 first-attempt trial added `nitro()`, hit all four breakages, reverted. `nitro` package stays in package.json for forward-compat (when TanStack Start improves PWA + LHCI integration in a future version). `npm run start` invokes the wrapper.
- Frontend Wave 131 SW2 /healthz fast-path: `frontend/src/server.ts` early-return BEFORE the `requestAuthStorage.run()` AsyncLocalStorage chain. URL parse (cheap); if `pathname === "/healthz"`, return static `{"status":"ok"}` JSON Response with `cache-control: no-store` + content-type. Response init prepared at module load time (`HEALTHZ_RESPONSE_BODY` + `HEALTHZ_RESPONSE_INIT` constants). Returns in <10ms — no JWT validation, no theme/lang cookie parse, no router construction. For Caddy `health_uri /healthz` + k8s livenessProbe / readinessProbe (typically 5s timeout). Chosen over `createFileRoute("/healthz")` route file: spa-mode build doesn't wire TanStack Start server-route handlers + early-return is structurally simpler (~5 LoC) and faster.
- Frontend Wave 131 SW3 frontend.Dockerfile Node SSR runtime: replaces `nginxinc/nginx-unprivileged:1.28.2-alpine` runtime with `node:24-alpine` (SHA-pinned, same digest as base stage). NEW `prod-deps` stage between `deps` and `runtime` runs `npm ci --omit=dev --legacy-peer-deps` after `npm pkg delete scripts.prepare scripts.postinstall` (drops husky `prepare` — devDep, exit code 127 under --omit=dev — and setup-lhci-binaries.cjs `postinstall` — ~50 MB Chrome download for LHCI testing). `preinstall` (ensure-wasm.mjs creates placeholder pkg/ dirs for wasm-sanitizer + rust-crypto file:// runtime deps) preserved. Runtime stage: COPY package.json + production node_modules from prod-deps, COPY frontend/scripts/server-prod.mjs, COPY dist/ from builder, USER node (UID/GID 1000 built into node:alpine images), ENV NODE_ENV=production / PORT=3000 / HOST=0.0.0.0, EXPOSE 3000, HEALTHCHECK on /healthz (15s start-period, 30s interval, 5s timeout, 3 retries via wget --quiet --tries=1 --spider), CMD `["node", "scripts/server-prod.mjs"]`. `frontend/nginx.conf` no longer referenced from Dockerfile but kept in source tree as documentation of prior production routing (W132+ polish candidate to delete).
- Frontend Wave 131 SW4 Caddy SSR routing: `infrastructure/Caddyfile` (mounted by docker-compose.full.yml) — default `handle { reverse_proxy frontend:3000 { health_uri /healthz; ... } }` (was `frontend:8080` nginx) + NEW explicit `handle /sw.js { reverse_proxy frontend:3000; header Service-Worker-Allowed "/"; header Cache-Control "no-store, no-cache, must-revalidate, max-age=0" }`. `services/caddy/Caddyfile` (production design with HTTP/3 + ratelimit plugin) — pre-W131 had a single root-level `reverse_proxy backend:8000` (internal-only configuration); SW4 introduces explicit multi-service routing matrix: /api/* → backend:8000 (X-Forwarded-* headers + healthz), /graphql* → backend:8000, /ws/* → ws-hub:8081 (rate_limit @ws_upgrade preserved), /static/* → backend:8000, /sw.js → frontend:3000, default → frontend:3000 (Node SSR + healthz). Existing Alt-Svc HTTP/3 + Strict-Transport-Security + log block preserved. `infrastructure/Caddyfile` validated via `docker run caddy:2.11.2-alpine caddy validate` → "Valid configuration".
- Frontend Wave 131 SW5 k8s/frontend Node SSR alignment: `deployment.yaml` containerPort 8080→3000, livenessProbe + readinessProbe httpGet path `/`→`/healthz`, initialDelaySeconds 10→15 (Node startup ~5-10s vs ~3s nginx), preStop sleep 5→10 (matches server-prod.mjs SIGTERM 30s drain), removed nginx-cache + nginx-run emptyDir mounts (no nginx in Node), kept tmp emptyDir (Node + V8 + libuv may write to /tmp; readOnlyRootFilesystem requires explicit writable mount). Resource bumps: cpu request 50m→100m / limit 200m→500m (SSR is CPU-bound vs nginx file IO); memory request 64Mi→128Mi / limit 256Mi→512Mi (Node + V8 heap + per-request SSR work peaks 250-400 MB under load). `network-policy.yaml` ingress port 8080→3000; egress unchanged (still gateway:8080 for /api proxy + DNS to kube-system). HPA + PDB unchanged.
- Frontend Wave 131 SW6 cookie SameSite=Lax migration: `app/core/config/mixins/csp_settings.py` `cookie_samesite` property — pre-W131 returned "lax" for dev / "strict" for prod; post-W131 both default to "lax". Strict blocked `access_token_v2` / `csrf_token` cookies on cross-site GET (direct link clicks from search engines, email, social media) — defeating SSR auth-at-edge. NEW `security_cookie_samesite_override: str = ""` (env var `SECURITY_COOKIE_SAMESITE_OVERRIDE`) provides emergency rollback knob; validator constrains to {"", "strict", "lax", "none"} at config-load time + lowercase normalization. CSRF middleware (`app/core/csrf.py`) preserved — Signed Double-Submit + HMAC-SHA256 + X-CSRF-Token header check (RZ-3, audit Mar 2026); Lax does not open new attack surface (CORS preflight blocks cross-site header forgery). NEW `tests/test_wave131_cookie_migration.py` — 8 unit tests targeting production `app.core.config.security.SecuritySettings` (NOT the legacy `app/config/security.py` shadow module which has its own simpler SecuritySettings without CspSettingsMixin). Tests: default_is_lax, override_strict_restores_pre_w131, override_lax_explicit, override_none_for_cross_site_embeds, override_empty_falls_through, override_case_insensitive (STRICT → strict), override_invalid_value_raises (RZ-131-01), csrf_middleware_cookie_uses_lax_default (end-to-end via CSRFMiddleware._build_set_cookie_header).
- Frontend Wave 131 SW7 server-prod.mjs static-files fix: caught at SW7 verification — tanstackStart's `dist/server/server.js` default export ONLY renders routes; static assets (`/assets/*`, `/favicon.ico`, `/sw.js`, `/manifest.webmanifest`, `/icon-*.png`, `/maskable-icon-*.png`, `/offline.html`, `/registerSW.js`, `/static-shell-i18n.js`) returned 404 from W131 SW1 wrapper. `vite preview` papers over this in dev because vite's preview-server has its own static file middleware ahead of the tanstackStart preview-server-plugin. Production via server-prod.mjs needs the static layer explicitly. Fix: static-first request flow added — GET/HEAD with non-root path tries `dist/client/<path>` first via `fs.statSync` + `createReadStream`; if file exists, respond with appropriate content-type + cache headers; if no static match, delegate to `handler.fetch(request)` for SSR routing. Cache headers: `/assets/*` + `/fonts/*` (Vite-hashed, immutable) → `cache-control: public, max-age=31536000, immutable`; `/sw.js` + `/registerSW.js` → `cache-control: no-store, ...` + `service-worker-allowed: /`; everything else → `cache-control: no-cache`. Path traversal defense: `path.resolve(staticRoot, requested)` + explicit `startsWith(staticRoot)` prefix check after `decodeURIComponent` (handles URL-encoded `%2e%2e` traversal). Verified `curl /../etc/passwd` → 404 (handler.fetch falls through, tanstackStart route NotFound).
- Frontend Wave 131 SW8 PROD bundle baseline (BYTE-IDENTICAL to W130): `dist/client/assets/index-KalQn95O.js` 138,974 bytes + `_shell.html` 65,872 bytes — confirms all W131 changes are server-side / infrastructure-only, ZERO client bundle impact. SW1+SW2+SW7 server.ts + server-prod.mjs changes are server-side only. SW3 Dockerfile, SW4 Caddy, SW5 k8s, SW6 csp_settings are all configuration-level — none touch the React client tree.

- Wave 132 SW1-fix docker-compose.full.yml port mismatch (W131 SW3 carry-over): W131 SW3 changed frontend.Dockerfile EXPOSE 8080→3000 + Node listening on 3000 (server-prod.mjs default), but `docker-compose.full.yml` frontend service kept stale `ports: 8081:8080` + healthcheck against `:8080/`. Compose started container successfully but :8081 mapped to nothing listening internally; healthcheck always failed (probed wrong port); container marked perpetually unhealthy. This is exactly why W131 §Honesty probe #1+#2 deferred Docker stack verification — the compose file had a real bug masked by the deferral. SW1-fix: `ports: 8081:3000` + healthcheck `wget http://127.0.0.1:3000/healthz` (W131 SW2 fast-path, <10ms). Caddy in same compose network reverse-proxies `frontend:3000` correctly via service-name DNS — only the host-mapped :8081 was broken. Future similar carry-overs would benefit from CI lint step that grep-checks compose port mappings against Dockerfile EXPOSE directives.

- Wave 132 SW3 Caddy `lb_policy weighted_round_robin` SPA-fallback canary syntax: in `services/caddy/Caddyfile` `reverse_proxy { ... }` block, use `to <upstream1> <upstream2>` (space-separated upstreams) + `lb_policy weighted_round_robin <weight1> <weight2>` (space-separated weights matching upstream order). Per Caddy 2.x docs `weighted_round_robin` is native (no plugin needed); weight `0` excludes that upstream entirely from selection. W132 SW3 ships initial weights `100 0` (stable=100, ssr=0) so the first apply at canary Stage 0 is an *identity change* — stable still serves all traffic; SSR pool excluded. Operator triggers canary via runbook command flip (edit weight values + ConfigMap update + `kubectl rollout restart deployment caddy` for ~50ms hot reload, no Pod cycling). `infrastructure/Caddyfile` (docker-compose mount) intentionally NOT changed — canary is operational, not local-test. Both pools use `health_uri /healthz`; the legacy nginx pool requires operator to add `/healthz` endpoint to its nginx config during canary prep (runbook step 4). Passive `fail_duration 30s unhealthy_status 5xx` adds reactive failure handling on top of active probes.

- Wave 132 SW4 k8s SPA-fallback canary template: `k8s/frontend/canary/deployment-stable.yaml` is a TEMPLATE (operator sets `${LEGACY_SPA_IMAGE}` placeholder during canary prep — e.g. `registry.example.com/frontend-stable:pre-w125-spa`). Manifests carry `app.kubernetes.io/name: frontend-stable` (distinct selector — existing `frontend` Service unchanged), `app.kubernetes.io/part-of: frontend` (groups stable + ssr in dashboards for per-pool comparison), `wave: w132-canary` (operator grep-and-delete at Stage 6 cleanup). Existing `k8s/frontend/deployment.yaml` adds explicit `spec.strategy.type: RollingUpdate` with `maxSurge: 25%`, `maxUnavailable: 0`, `progressDeadlineSeconds: 600` — `maxUnavailable: 0` ensures NO drop in capacity during canary stage Pod cycling. Resource ceiling for `frontend-stable` pinned at pre-W131 nginx values (cpu 50m/200m, memory 64Mi/256Mi) since nginx is much lighter than Node SSR. `k8s/frontend/canary/canary-flip-strategies.md` (NEW ~120 lines) is operator quick-reference for per-stage progression table + flip commands + rollback.

- Wave 132 SW5 Server-Timing middleware in server-prod.mjs: emits `Server-Timing: ssr;dur=<float>;desc="ssr-render"` per SSR response where `dur` is `performance.now()` measurement of `handler.fetch(request)` (tanstackStart router + matched-route render + loader chain). `pipeWebResponse(webResponse, res, extraHeaders?)` accepts optional 3rd arg; values appended via `res.appendHeader` (Node 18+) so we don't clobber upstream Set-Cookie / content-type. Skipped for /healthz (W131 SW2 fast-path stays clean — probes shouldn't be tagged as SSR-pool traffic) and static assets (W131 SW7 `serveStatic` short-circuits before SSR layer). Operators identify SSR-pool responses during canary by header presence (filter responses by `Server-Timing IS NOT NULL` in any HTTP-aware analyzer); aggregated `dur` distribution per stage feeds advance/abort decisions. Verified locally via curl: /login emits header (cold-start 662ms), /healthz + /assets/* don't. PROD client bundle BYTE-IDENTICAL to W131 (server-prod.mjs is Node script, not bundled into React client tree).

- Wave 132 SW6 canary rollout runbook at `docs/CANARY_ROLLOUT_PHASE6.md` (DEPLOY.md naming convention, NOT new docs/operations/ directory): comprehensive ~447-line operator runbook for the W125-W131 SSR migration arc canary rollout. 8 sections: at-a-glance + architecture map ASCII diagram; prerequisites (cluster + registry + monitoring + W131 SECURITY_COOKIE_SAMESITE_OVERRIDE rollback knob); pre-canary checklist (8 steps: W131 baseline verify, LHCI baseline capture, deployment.yaml apply, build legacy SPA fallback image with /healthz endpoint, apply frontend-stable manifest, apply Caddy config Stage 0 identity change, smoke-test infra, capture Stage 0 baseline metrics 24h); canary stages 1-5 (per-stage advance/abort criteria placeholders flagged, weight-flip command pattern, soak monitoring queries, Server-Timing observation); rollback procedure (instant Caddy weight flip back to `100 0`) + 8-failure-mode catalog with W131/W132 mitigations mapped (hydration mismatch → W127 SW2-5 cookie-mirror; SameSite=Lax SSO → SECURITY_COOKIE_SAMESITE_OVERRIDE=strict; SW collision → W131 SW4 single-source /sw.js; VITE_LHCI bypass leak → pre-deploy `grep -l "lhci-mock-user"`; push subscription invalidation → 24-48h soak observation; Caddy hot-reload 5xx → expected ~50-200ms; Node SSR memory leak → k8s memory limit + scheduled restart; ws-hub reconnect storm → preStop sleep:10 + ws-hub backoff); Stage 6 cleanup (delete frontend-stable, revert single-upstream Caddy, re-baseline LHCI gates per W124 SW4 ratchet method); known limitations + carry-forward deferrals (8 items honestly listed); cross-references; lessons-learned template (operator fills post-Phase-6). Threshold values explicitly marked as PLACEHOLDERS — operator captures real Stage 0 baseline + adjusts before Stage 1.

- Wave 132 SW7 SW1 partial-closure honest framing: per `feedback_perfectionism.md` "if you can't measure, defer honestly" pattern, the Docker stack runtime verification attempt to close W131 §Honesty probe #1+#2 was killed mid-build (cargo wasm-pack compile phase — structurally slow on Windows fresh build, ~30+ min sequential dep compile). W132 SW7 documents partial closure: **runtime-level** verified (Node SSR runs cleanly via `npm run start` on host; Server-Timing emits correctly; static assets served per W131 SW7; healthz fast-path; SSR routes; auth-at-edge 307 redirect), **integration-level NOT verified** (full Caddy → Node SSR → backend chain through real Docker stack stays W133+ scope). The `docker-compose.full.yml` SW1-fix carry-over IS shipped + structurally correct — operator can `docker compose -f docker-compose.full.yml up -d` and the chain should work. Future wave (or operator with cluster access) can complete the integration verification via Phase 6 actual rollout per W132 SW6 runbook.

- Wave 133 SW1 SSR cookie forwarding via 4th AsyncLocalStorage (`requestCookieStorage`) + axios request-interceptor branch: extends W126 SW3 + W127 SW4 pattern — `frontend/src/server.ts` adds `requestCookieStorage = new AsyncLocalStorage<string>()` parallel to existing 3 storages (auth/theme/lang); `globalThis.__ssrCookieGetter__: (() => string | undefined) | undefined` getter mirrors `__ssrAuthGetter__`; raw `Cookie` header read via `request.headers.get("cookie") ?? ""` at top of fetch handler; 4th-level `.run()` in nesting chain (auth → cookie → theme → lang → handler.fetch). Client-side: `frontend/src/api/client.ts` request interceptor augmented with SSR-only branch gated by `typeof window === "undefined" && globalThis.__ssrCookieGetter__?.()?.length > 0` — sets `Cookie` header on outgoing config via `AxiosHeaders.from(config.headers ?? {}).set("Cookie", cookie)`. **Tree-shake invariant (refined at W133 polish pass)**: `requestCookieStorage` AsyncLocalStorage instance stays server-only (verified `grep -l "requestCookieStorage" dist/client/assets/*.js` → 0 matches; Vite environments build correctly partitions). The CONSUMER reference (`globalThis.__ssrCookieGetter__?.()` in client.ts interceptor) IS in the `client-*.js` chunk as ~30 bytes of runtime-dead code via the `typeof window` runtime gate (NOT statically dead — runtime check). This is BY DESIGN — the consumer must ship in the shared module to fire on Node SSR. **SECURITY: NEVER log or surface `globalThis.__ssrCookieGetter__()` raw value** — contains the access_token_v2 HttpOnly cookie + csrf_token + ue-mode + ue:language mirror cookies. Server.ts line ~119 inline comment + audit doc. Bundle delta from W132 baseline 138,974 → W133 139,549 = **+575 bytes** main chunk (forensic at polish: NOT from cookie interceptor in main — it's in `client-*.js` chunk; main delta from modulepreload graph + import bookkeeping for new dependency edges); honest framing per `feedback_perfectionism.md` — NOT byte-identical, real client-tree weight.

- Wave 133 SW2 `currentUserQueryOptions()` factory placement convention: NEW `frontend/src/api/hooks/users.ts` mirrors W130 SW1 schedule.ts factory shape exactly. `currentUserQueryKey: readonly ["users", "me"]` + factory returning `{ queryKey, queryFn: ({ signal }) => fetchCurrentUser({ signal }), staleTime: 60_000, gcTime: 5*60_000, networkMode: "online", retry: 2, retryDelay }`. queryFn delegates to existing `fetchCurrentUser` (`useProfileSync.ts:485-515`) — preserves cache-envelope header logic + retry-on-500-with-cleared-cache behavior. NO duplication. Used by SSR loaders (`/schedule` + `/profile` + `/settings` per W133 SW3-5); `useProfileSync` continues calling `fetchCurrentUser` directly for synchronous-bootstrap-then-async-init lifecycle (migration to React Query own-wave per W133 §Honesty #3). Pattern extends W129 events.ts + news.ts + W130 schedule.ts + weather.ts placement convention.

- Wave 133 SW3 sequential SSR loader pattern: `routes/_auth/schedule.tsx` loader for the canonical "data depends on prior fetch" pattern. Phase 1 parallel via `Promise.allSettled([currentUserQueryOptions, scheduleGroupsQueryOptions])`; phase 2 conditional on `userResult.status === "fulfilled" && userResult.value && typeof userResult.value.group_id === "string"`: `await context.queryClient.ensureQueryData(pageScheduleQueryOptions(group_id)).catch(() => undefined)`. `.catch(() => undefined)` is symmetric with W128 SW3-followup `Promise.all → Promise.allSettled` pattern at /dashboard — best-effort phase-2 fetch shouldn't block the loader (which would render blank → NO_FCP). Future routes that need user-derived data (e.g. user-specific search filters, user-specific feed personalization) can reuse this template. **Caveat under VITE_LHCI bypass**: the LHCI mock user has no `group_id`, so phase 2 doesn't fire — /schedule SSR HTML stays at ~70K bytes (no lessons rendered server-side). Real users with group_id WILL see SSR-rendered lessons. Verification path: chrome-devtools-mcp through real Docker chain (W134+ Option B) OR augment LHCI mock with group_id.

- Wave 133 SW4-5 minimal /users/me prefetch as SSR-enable strategy: routes opting INTO SSR can do so cheaply (~6 LoC delta) by removing the W128 SW2 explicit `ssr: false`, inheriting parent `_auth.tsx ssr:true`, and adding `loader: async ({ context }) => Promise.allSettled([context.queryClient.ensureQueryData(currentUserQueryOptions())])`. Bounded LCP win: shell + auth-aware nav rendered server-side; subpage content remains client. Used by /profile + /settings in W133 SW4-5. Pre-W133 baseline (~10K shell) → post-W133 SSR HTML 69,174 b /profile + 79,889 b /settings. Pattern recipe for any authenticated route whose primary above-the-fold content depends on /users/me.

- Wave 133 chrome-devtools-mcp `navigate_page` 30s timeout vs `new_page` workaround on backend-down (extends W129 §Honesty pattern): when verifying SSR HTML rendering against `vite preview` without a running backend, `navigate_page` (existing tab navigation) waits indefinitely for component-level useEffect-driven backend fetches that never resolve. Use `new_page` instead — opens a fresh tab + completes when SSR HTML is delivered + initial hydration starts. Console messages still readable even if full hydration completion blocks on backend. Documented in W129 §Honesty + replicated in W133 SW6 verification (only `new_page` succeeded for /profile + /settings; `navigate_page` timed out at 30 s). Expect future SSR routes to need same approach.

- Wave 133 setupTests.ts SSR-safety guards as test-infra improvement: `frontend/src/setupTests.ts` augmented with `typeof window !== "undefined"` guard around polyfill block (matchMedia + IntersectionObserver + ResizeObserver) and `typeof document !== "undefined"` guard around `document.documentElement.lang = "en"` in beforeAll. Backwards-compatible — jsdom branch unchanged (`typeof window === "object"` so polyfills still apply). Required for node-environment vitest tests (W133 SW1 ssrCookie.test.ts uses `@vitest-environment node` directive); without these guards setupTests crashes on `window is not defined` during module load. Matches W113 SW6 jsdom-polyfill cleanup pattern. Cost: ~12 lines + 1 nesting level.

- Wave 134 SW1 useProfileSync Bridge mechanism (`feat(wave134-sw1-useprofilesync-bridge)` `496eb5bbf`): closes W133 §Honesty #3+#4 by routing useProfileSync's auto-fetch effect through `queryClient.fetchQuery({...currentUserQueryOptions(), retry: false})` instead of calling `fetchCurrentUser({signal})` directly. Eliminates duplicated /users/me network calls between SSR loaders and useProfileSync's auto-fetch on cold-load of /dashboard, /profile, /settings, /schedule. `currentUserQueryKey = ["users", "me"]` was already defined in BOTH `useProfileSync.ts:58` AND `users.ts:52` (W133 SW2) — same tuple, same cache slot; bridge eliminates the duplicated network call by routing the auto-fetch through that shared cache. **`retry: false` override is critical**: factory's default `retry: 2 + retryDelay` would delay 401 propagation by ~3s and break loading-flag transition timing — `AuthContext.test.tsx:177 "discards tampered cached envelopes"` was the canary (failed initially, passes with retry: false override). `queryClient.cancelQueries({queryKey: currentUserQueryKey})` added alongside existing `controller.abort()` for cancellation parity. Bundle delta -194 bytes (favourable; tree-shake removed redundant fetchCurrentUser closures now that auto-fetch routes through factory's queryFn delegate). 4 integration tests in `frontend/src/contexts/__tests__/AuthContext.bridge.test.tsx`.

- Wave 134 SW2 sessionsQueryOptions factory + /settings tab=N URL param (`feat(wave134-sw2-settings-tab-url-sessions-prefetch)` `84487ed09`): closes W133 §Honesty #1 (per-subpage data prefetch deferred to W134+) for the highest-value sub-target — the /settings Security tab's sessions list. NEW `frontend/src/api/hooks/sessions.ts` exports `sessionsQueryOptions(userId)` factory + `sessionsQueryKey(userId)` returning `["auth", "sessions", userId]` (mirrors W129/W130/W133 placement convention). NEW `frontend/src/features/settings/schema.ts` exports `settingsSearchSchema` Valibot with `{tab, spotify}` fields + `SETTINGS_TAB.{GENERAL, ACCOUNT, SECURITY, INTEGRATIONS}` constants. tab uses W120 SW5 number-or-numeric-string union pattern (TanStack Router's default `stringifySearch` JSON-quotes numeric strings as `?tab="2"`). Range 0-3 + `v.fallback(..., 0)` for invalid input. /settings route loader uses `loaderDeps: ({search}) => ({tab: search.tab})` + conditional `ensureQueryData(sessionsQueryOptions(userResult.id))` when `deps.tab === SETTINGS_TAB.SECURITY` — TanStack Router v1's canonical pattern for loader-side search access (verified via `@tanstack/router-core route.d.ts:loaderDeps?: (opts: FullSearchSchemaOption) => TLoaderDeps`). `useSessionManagement` refactored to spread the factory (preserves public API + cache-identity sessionsKey). Settings.tsx tab state lifted from useState to URL-derived (`search.tab ?? 0`); setTab navigates with `replace: true + viewTransition: false` (FIX-77-03). `useSearch({strict: false})` preserved for component-level test mountability. 9 sessions factory tests + 20 schema tests added.

- Wave 134 schema fallback subtle behaviour (Valibot v.fallback wrapping v.optional): `parse({}).tab` returns 0 (key entirely missing → fallback fires), but `parse({tab: undefined}).tab` returns undefined (optional accepts undefined directly without invoking fallback). Settings.tsx applies `?? 0` consumer-side default to catch both paths consistently. Documented in `frontend/src/features/settings/__tests__/schema.test.ts` test names + schema doc string. Future Valibot schemas using the same `v.fallback(v.optional(...), default)` pattern should expect this divergence and apply consumer-side `?? default` for uniformity.

- Wave 134 SW3 MEMORY.md compaction strategy (no git commit; memory file at `C:\Users\egorribun\.claude\projects\C--Users-egorribun-Documents-university-ecosystem\memory\MEMORY.md` is in user .claude profile, not repo-tracked): pre-W134 75,851 bytes / 184 lines fired auto-load truncation warning every session (limit 24.4 KB). Compacted approach: keep most-recent 3 closed waves verbose in BOTH "## Active backlog" + "## Audit History" sections; collapse W115-W130 to one-line entries with link to detail audit doc; preserve Stack/Patterns/Conventions/Docker/Maturity sections verbatim (concise enough). Result: 21,140 bytes / 160 lines (-72%; -24 lines). Closes the auto-load warning that affected EVERY session since wave history accumulated past 24.4 KB threshold. The convention going forward: when MEMORY.md grows back near 24 KB (typically every 8-12 waves of audit accumulation), repeat the compaction by collapsing the next batch of older verbose entries.

- Wave 135 SW1 AbortController removal in useProfileSync (`feat(wave135-sw1-cleanup-aggressive)` `6c5ada141`): closes W134 §Honesty #3 by routing all cancellation through `queryClient.cancelQueries({ queryKey: currentUserQueryKey })`. `activeRequestRef = useRef<AbortController | null>(null)` removed (was line 720); `clearProfile`'s `controller?.abort()` replaced with `queryClient.cancelQueries`; auto-fetch effect's `new AbortController()` block removed; outer catch `controller.signal.aborted` → `isCancel(error)` (axios canonical, sets `error.code === "ERR_CANCELED"`); `finally` block collapsed to single `setInitializing(false)` (idempotent under React batching). `import { ..., isCancel } from "axios"` added. Pre-W135 silent-skip behaviour on cancellation preserved via isCancel guard. Future cancellation in this hook should use `queryClient.cancelQueries({ queryKey: currentUserQueryKey })` only.

- Wave 135 SW1 sessions factory mutation helpers (`feat(wave135-sw1-cleanup-aggressive)` `6c5ada141`): closes W134 §Honesty #5 by exporting `updateSessionInCache(queryClient, userId, updated)` + `invalidateSessions(queryClient, userId)` from `frontend/src/api/hooks/sessions.ts`. Mirrors W129 events.ts / W130 schedule.ts factory placement convention. `useSessionManagement` mutation paths (revokeSession + revokeAllSessions) route through these helpers; `sessionsKey = useMemo(...)` removed (factory derives key from primitive userId). Defensive: `if (!Array.isArray(previous)) return previous` no-op when cache slot empty or hydrated to non-array. Future mutation cache writes for the sessions slot should use these helpers, NOT inline `setQueryData(sessionsKey, ...)`. 11 new tests in `frontend/src/api/hooks/__tests__/sessions.test.ts`.

Subprocess `vite build` (CLI not programmatic — programmatic exits cleanly but doesn't fire tanstackStart prerender) with kill-after-artifacts pattern: poll for `_shell.html` AND `dist/server/server.js` to exist + be stable for 4 × 500ms ticks (2s debounce), then SIGTERM. esbuild compiles `src/sw.ts` → `dist/client/sw.js` (53,181 bytes; respects tsconfig `@/*` → `src/*` paths). `workbox-build.injectManifest` standalone replaces `self.__WB_MANIFEST` placeholder with actual precache manifest array (209 files / 4.80 MB). post-build-shell.mjs unchanged (CSP nonce + font preload + LHCI placeholder + mirror to index.html). Build × 3 reproducible 26s/run (vs wave127 ~95s/run). Honest §Honesty: kill-after-artifacts is improvement NOT structural fix; second hang point in tanstackStart-core remains W136 candidate. CI Linux + dev mode unaffected (BUILD_SKIP_PWA falsy → VitePWA enabled normally; subprocess exits cleanly without kill needed). `npm run build:legacy` preserves run-build.mjs as rollback.

Real culprits per `layout-shift-elements` LHCI audit: (a) footer position (`body.dark > div#root > div.flex > footer.bg-footer` shifts as body grows), (b) `div.fixed` inside `main#main-content` (NotificationsPermissionPrompt / tips card renders late), (c) SkeletonMorph swap (skeleton height ≠ content height). These are **content layout shifts**, not animation shifts — Wave 117 SWs did NOT address them. Deferred to Wave 118 SW1 (own-wave per `memory/wave118_backlog.md`). SW3-SW6 landed real wins: OTEL chunk split (SW3), /news LCP 1430→1363ms via css-stagger migration (SW4 mirrors PERF-77-01), picsum.photos preconnect + dns-prefetch (SW5 — LCPDiscovery FAILED on /news since URL is seeded client-side, preconnect pre-warms TCP+TLS+DNS), mobile `@media (max-width: 640px)` halved backdrop-filter blur on surface/elevated/floating/matte layers (SW6 — saturate multiplier preserved, aesthetic holds).
